_worker_doc_titles\n    _worker_idf = idf\n    _worker_inverted_index = inverted_index\n    _worker_doc_vectors = doc_vectors\n    _worker_doc_norms = doc_norms\n    _worker_doc_titles = doc_titles\n\n\ndef _search_single_query(args: tuple) -> list[tuple[int, float, str]]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for documents similar to a single query.\n    Uses global worker state initialized by _init_search_worker.\n\n    Args:\n        args: Tuple of (query, top_k)\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (doc_id, score, title) tuples\n    \"\"\"\n    query, top_k = args\n\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in _worker_idf:\n            tfidf = tf * _worker_idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in _worker_inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in _worker_inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = _worker_doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = _worker_doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    top_results = nlargest(top_k, scores, key=lambda x: x[1])\n\n    # Return lightweight tuples\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = _worker_doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\") if _worker_doc_titles else f\"Document {doc_id}\"\n        results.append((doc_id, score, title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef _search_query_batch(args: tuple) -> list[list[tuple[int, float, str]]]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for a batch of queries.\n    Uses global worker state initialized by _init_search_worker.\n\n    Args:\n        args: Tuple of (queries, top_k)\n\n    Returns:\n        List of result lists (each is list of (doc_id, score, title) tuples)\n    \"\"\"\n    queries, top_k = args\n    results = []\n    for query in queries:\n        results.append(_search_single_query((query, top_k)))\n    return results\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Main Parallel Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document],\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    chunk_size: int = 500,\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    The parallelization strategy focuses on the document processing phase,\n    which is the most computationally intensive and embarrassingly parallel.\n    Later phases have data dependencies that limit parallelization benefits.\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (defaults to CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk for parallel processing\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = cpu_count()\n\n    # Ensure reasonable worker count\n    num_workers = max(1, min(num_workers, cpu_count(), len(documents) // 100 + 1))\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Step 1: Parallel tokenization and TF computation\n    # Prepare lightweight data for workers (avoid serializing full Document objects)\n    docs_data = [(doc.doc_id, doc.title, doc.content) for doc in documents]\n\n    # Create chunks for parallel processing\n    chunks = [\n        docs_data[i : i + chunk_size] for i in range(0, len(docs_data), chunk_size)\n    ]\n\n    doc_term_freqs: dict[int, dict[str, float]] = {}\n\n    # Use ProcessPoolExecutor for better resource management\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=num_workers) as executor:\n        chunk_results = list(executor.map(_process_documents_batch, chunks))\n\n    # Collect results and build vocabulary\n    for chunk_result in chunk_results:\n        for doc_id, tf in chunk_result:\n            doc_term_freqs[doc_id] = tf\n            index.vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    # Step 2: Calculate document frequencies (sequential - fast with optimized data structure)\n    # Use Counter approach which is more efficient\n    doc_freq_counter = defaultdict(int)\n    for tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.values():\n        for term in tf_dict:\n            doc_freq_counter[term] += 1\n    index.document_frequencies = dict(doc_freq_counter)\n\n    # Step 3: Compute IDF scores (sequential, very fast - O(vocabulary))\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 4: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential with optimization)\n    # Pre-group documents by terms for efficient posting list construction\n    term_postings = defaultdict(list)\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            term_postings[term].append((doc_id, tfidf))\n\n    # Sort posting lists by score descending\n    for term in term_postings:\n        term_postings[term].sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n\n    index.inverted_index = dict(term_postings)\n\n    # Step 5: Build document vectors and compute norms (sequential - memory efficient)\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n    )\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] = None,\n) -> tuple[list[list[SearchResult]], float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for multiple queries in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Args:\n        queries: List of search query strings\n        index: TF-IDF index to search\n        top_k: Number of top results per query\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (defaults to CPU count)\n        documents: Optional list of documents for title lookup\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (results_list, elapsed_time) where results_list is a list of\n        SearchResult lists (one per query)\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = cpu_count()\n\n    # Ensure reasonable worker count\n    num_workers = max(1, min(num_workers, cpu_count(), len(queries)))\n\n    # Prepare document titles (lightweight data)\n    doc_titles = {d.doc_id: d.title for d in documents} if documents else {}\n\n    # Divide queries into batches for each worker\n    # This reduces IPC overhead by sending fewer larger messages\n    batch_size = max(1, (len(queries) + num_workers - 1) // num_workers)\n    query_batches = [\n        (queries[i:i + batch_size], top_k)\n        for i in range(0, len(queries), batch_size)\n    ]\n\n    # Use pool with initializer to share index across workers\n    # Index is serialized once per worker during initialization\n    with Pool(\n        processes=num_workers,\n        initializer=_init_search_worker,\n        initargs=(index.idf, index.inverted_index, index.doc_vectors, index.doc_norms, doc_titles),\n    ) as pool:\n        batch_results = pool.map(_search_query_batch, query_batches)\n\n    # Flatten and convert tuples back to SearchResult objects\n    results = []\n    for batch_result in batch_results:\n        for query_result in batch_result:\n            results.append([\n                SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title)\n                for doc_id, score, title in query_result\n            ])\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return results, elapsed\n\n\n#
